Important Milestone 6 rules 
Circle geometry shall remain center-based: CenterX, CenterY, and Radius are the authoritative geometry 
variables. 
A circle resize shall always preserve one common Radius value. Separate width and height values are 
not allowed for a true circle. 
Every accepted move or resize shall immediately recalculate derived circle geometry before rendering 
and before persistence. 
Protection Padding is used for overlap and containment validation. Hover Padding is used only for hover 
detection and visual activation. 
Saving shall persist committed circle values only. Temporary hover, drag, resize, and invalid-candidate 
state shall not be saved. 
Circle move and resize completion events shall be exposed so Milestone 7 can later recalculate attached 
junction points and connection lines. 
Mathematical Calculation Rules for Milestone 6 
Milestone 6 calculations control circle hover, selection, move, resize, derived-geometry recalculation, and 
committed circle persistence. This milestone does not create connection lines or connection lookup 
behavior. 
Primary JavaScript files for this calculation scope: circle-hover-hit-test.js, circle-selection.js, compute-
circle-candidate-center-on-move.js, circle-resize-control-point-hit-test.js, resize-circle.js, recalculate-circle-
derived-geometry-on-resize.js, circle-overlap-on-move.js, restore-previous-valid-position.js. 
Circle hover and selection 
DistanceSquared = (PointerX - CircleCenterX)^2 + (PointerY - CircleCenterY)^2. 
Pointer is in the circle hover band when Radius^2 <= DistanceSquared <= HoverPaddedRadius^2. 
Circle move calculation 
CandidateCenterX = PreviousCenterX + DeltaWorldX. CandidateCenterY = PreviousCenterY + 
DeltaWorldY. 
--- PAGE 333 ---
The candidate center shall be accepted only after Protection Padding non-overlap and containment 
validation pass. If validation fails, the circle shall be restored to the previous valid center. 
Circle resize calculation 
RightResizeControlPoint = (CenterX + Radius, CenterY). TopResizeControlPoint = (CenterX, CenterY + 
Radius). LeftResizeControlPoint = (CenterX - Radius, CenterY). BottomResizeControlPoint = (CenterX, 
CenterY - Radius). 
When a resize control point is dragged, NewRadius = distance(Center, DraggedWorldPoint), or the axis-
constrained equivalent for the selected control point. NewRadius must remain positive and must satisfy 
any minimum radius rule. 
After any accepted circle resize, recalculate Radius, protection padded radius, protection bounding box, 
hover region, and resize control point positions before rendering or saving. 
 
Milestone 6 phases 
Phase 6.1 — Circle hover detection and selection behavior  
Detect when the pointer enters the circle hover band, the circle body, or a circle resize control point. A left-
click on a valid circle target selects the circle. Hover state is temporary and shall not be persisted. 
Success criteria: circle hover and selection are visually clear, stable across zoom levels, and based on 
world-space hit testing. 
Phase 6.2 — Move the selected circle on DiagramCanvas 
Move the selected circle by updating CenterX and CenterY in world space. Movement shall validate 
against applicable overlap and containment rules, then either apply the candidate center or restore the 
previous valid center. 
Success criteria: accepted circle movement updates CenterX and CenterY only, recalculates protection 
geometry, renders immediately, and marks the diagram dirty. 
Phase 6.3 — Resize the circle from four resize control points 
The circle shall support RightResizeControlPoint, TopResizeControlPoint, LeftResizeControlPoint, and 
BottomResizeControlPoint. Dragging any resize control point changes Radius while keeping the circle 
circular. 
Success criteria: accepted resize updates Radius, recalculates all circle derived geometry, preserves 
minimum radius, and exposes a circle-resized event for later Milestone 7 connection recalculation. 
Phase 6.4 — Finalize circle interaction state and event handoff 
When a move or resize completes, temporary interaction state shall be cleared and one clean completion 
event shall be emitted. These events do not create connections by themselves; they only allow later 
milestones to respond. 
Event Trigger Expected later use 
--- PAGE 334 ---
circle-moved Accepted circle move 
completed. 
Milestone 7 may recalculate 
attached connection lines. 
circle-resized Accepted circle resize 
completed. 
Milestone 7 may recalculate 
all junction points attached to 
the circle. 
Phase 6.5 — Save circle-related committed changes to JSON 
Exact variables to save in this phase: CircleID, CenterX, CenterY , Radius, ZOrder, FillType, FillColor, 
LineType, LineColor, LineWidth, HoverPaddingRadiusRatio, HoverPaddingColor, 
ProtectionPaddingRadiusRatio, ProtectionPaddingColor, ParentContainerID when applicable. 
Save test criteria: save-to-json.js shall write the exact committed circle variable set and shall exclude 
temporary hover/drag/resize state. 
Phase 6.6 — Save circle-related committed changes to the database 
Exact variables to save in this phase are the same committed circle variables used in the JSON path. 
JavaScript shall send the committed circle payload to C#, and C# shall validate and persist it through the 
trusted backend path. 
Save test criteria: save-to-db.js and the C# persistence path shall store and reload the committed circle 
state without persisting temporary interaction-only variables. 
Phase 6.7 — Recalculate committed circle geometry and persist to both 
JSON and database 
Before a committed save is accepted, the system shall recalculate ProtectionPaddedRadius, protection 
bounding box, visible bounding box, hover radius, and resize control point positions from canonical circle 
variables. 
Success criteria: saved and reopened circle state produces the same visible circle, same protection 
geometry, and same interaction geometry. 
Final result after Milestone 6 
Circle hover, selection, movement, and resize are working. 
Circle geometry remains center-based and radius-based. 
Derived circle geometry recalculates after every accepted change. 
Circle move and circle resize completion hooks exist for later connection recalculation. 
JSON and database persistence include only committed circle state. 
Recommended implementation order 
Implement hover and selection. 
Implement movement and previous-valid-center behavior. 
Implement resize from four control points. 
--- PAGE 335 ---
Implement derived-geometry recalculation after accepted changes. 
Expose circle-moved and circle-resized events. 
Extend shared JSON and DB save files for committed circle state. 
Milestone 7 — Connecting Two Geometric Items Using Junction Points 
and Lines 
Overview 
Milestone 7 defines the complete connection behavior between two existing connectable geometries on 
DiagramCanvas. It covers Connect-To mode, connection-type lookup, connection-option display, junction-
point calculation, route calculation, connection styling, connection labels, resize/move recalculation, 
JSON persistence, and database persistence. 
The milestone assumes that rectangles, circles, containers, movement, resize behavior, and committed 
save/load foundations already exist. Milestone 7 does not create the source or destination geometries. It 
connects geometries that are already present in the active diagram model. 
Goal 
The goal of Milestone 7 is to let the user create, render, save, load, and recalculate a connection between 
two existing geometries using real source and destination Square Junction Point endpoints, while using 
route points for one-line and multi-link line geometry. 
The milestone must also support connection-type selection from CSV or database, default connection 
styling from Global Variables -> Connections, and database-driven rendering when connection rows 
already exist. 
Scope 
Milestone 7 includes the following work: 
• right-click target detection on an existing connectable geometry 
• geometry context menu display with a Connect To menu item 
• Connect-To mode activation and action locking 
• left-click selection of the second geometry as the target 
• CSV-first and database-fallback connection-type lookup 
• connection options menu display after the second geometry is selected 
• connection default style retrieval from Global Variables -> Connections 
• source and destination Square Junction Point calculation and creation 
• single-line shortest-path connection calculation 
• two-line, three-line, and four-line route point calculation 
• source junction text, destination junction text, and middle-line text placement 
--- PAGE 336 ---
• connection recalculation when connected geometries move or resize 
• JSON and database persistence of actual connections and connection details 
• database-driven reload or refresh of existing connections. 
Milestone 7 excludes the following work unless a later milestone explicitly adds it: 
• creating new devices or containers from the Shapes Panel 
• turning intermediate route bend points into real editable junction-point items 
• automatic obstacle-avoiding routing around unrelated geometries 
• connection animation, simulation, bandwidth calculation, or live network state 
• changing Global Variables defaults retroactively on old connections without an explicit apply-to-existing 
command. 
Prerequisites 
Before Milestone 7 starts, the following must already be available and verified:

Milestone 7 terminology 
Term Meaning 
Source geometry The first geometry selected by right -click before entering Connect -To mode. 
Target geometry The second geometry selected by left click while Connect -To mode is active. 
Connect-To mode Temporary interaction mode used to select a second geometry and choose a 
connection type. 
Connection type Business or visual meaning of the connection, such as IPsecTunnel, 
AWSVPCPeerLink, OneLine, TwoLines, ThreeLines, or FourLines.  
Connection route type The route calculation family, such as ShortestPathSingleLine, MultiLinkDelta, or 
BusinessDefined. 
Real Square Junction Point A real endpoint item with its own ID. Milestone 7 creates or restores one source 
junction point and one destination junction point.  
Derived route point An intermediate bend point calculated for multi -link rendering. It is not a real 
Square Junction Point by default.  
Source junction text Text displayed near the source junction point. 
Destination junction text Text displayed near the destination junction point. 
Middle-line text Text displayed around the midpoint of the rendered connection polyline.  
Host location parameter A normalized parameter that allows a junction point to be rebuilt on a rectangle 
edge or circle boundary after load or resize.  
 
Important milestone rules 
• All connection calculations shall run in world space. Screen coordinates must be converted to world 
coordinates before calculation. 
• Connect-To mode shall disable unrelated diagram actions while it is active. 
• The only allowed actions during Connect-To mode are selecting a valid second geometry, cancelling the 
mode, or closing the connection workflow. 
• The actual line route calculation shall not run until the user selects a connection option from the menu 
shown after the second geometry is selected. 
• CSV lookup has priority over database lookup. If CSV is unavailable or has no matching row, the 
database lookup table shall be used. 
• Connection-type lookup is bidirectional unless the lookup row explicitly says it is directional. 
--- PAGE 338 ---
• A Rectangle to Circle lookup row shall also support Circle to Rectangle without requiring a duplicate row. 
• Milestone 7 creates or restores exactly two real Square Junction Point endpoints for a normal 
connection: one source endpoint and one destination endpoint. 
• Intermediate multi-link route bend points are derived route points by default, not real Square Junction 
Point items. 
• Connection defaults from Global Variables -> Connections are copied into the actual connection detail 
record when the connection is created. 
• Changing a global connection default later shall not silently change old saved connections unless a 
future explicit apply-to-existing command is added. 
• DiagramConnections identifies what is connected to what. DiagramConnectionDetails stores route, 
style, junction, and label information. 
• ConnectionID is the common key between DiagramConnections and DiagramConnectionDetails. 
• All committed connection variables shall be added to save-to-json.js and save-to-db.js. Temporary 
interaction state shall not be saved as committed truth. 
Connect-To workflow 
1. right-click target detection 
2. opening the geometry context menu 
3. showing Connect To as a menu item 
4. entering Connect-To mode 
5. Connect-To mode disables all other diagram actions, including drag/drop of new diagrams or devices, 
moving geometries, resizing unrelated geometries, deleting geometries, pasting, duplicating, or starting 
another tool action 
6. left click selecting the second geometry as the target 
7. check connection types from either CSV or database; priority is CSV first, and if CSV is not available or 
does not contain a match, use database 
8. display the information retrieved from CSV or database in a menu once the second geometry has been 
selected 
9. the actual line calculation happens in the next phase after the user selects one connection option.

Bidirectional lookup rule: if the exact source-to-destination lookup is not found, the system shall check the 
swapped destination-to-source form unless the row is explicitly directional. Therefore, the table does not 
need a separate Circle to Rectangle row when Rectangle to Circle already exists. 
Global Variables — Connections tab 
The Global Variables window shall include a tab named Connections. Existing tabs such as Canvas 
background, Rectangle, and Circle shall remain unchanged. The Connections tab shall display the default 
visual line properties of each supported connection type. 
Connection type Default line type Default color Default thickness 
IPsec tunnel DottedLine Orange Thin 
AWS VPC peer link SolidLine Black Thick 
 
The right-side properties panel of the Connections tab shall show the editable default properties for each 
connection type. For IPsec tunnel, the defaults are dotted line, orange color, and thin line. For AWS VPC 
peer link, the defaults are solid line, black color, and thick line.

dashed lines. 
IsActive bit Whether this default connection 
type is available.

Every major Milestone 7 function shall be documented using the standard function contract already

Mathematical Calculation Rules for Milestone 7 
This section is the authoritative calculation scope for Milestone 7. All calculations shall use world-space 
values. Screen-space values are input/rendering values only and shall not be used as stored connection 
geometry truth. 
JavaScript calculation ownership

host parameter

HostEdgeName, 
HostLocationParameter 
Real endpoint object creation junction-point-factory.js SourceJunctionPointID and 
DestinationJunctionPointID 
Shortest source/destination 
endpoint selection

route points

list

positions only 
 
Canonical geometry inputs 
For an axis-aligned rectangle host R: 
R_Left   = R_CenterX - R_HalfWidth 
R_Right  = R_CenterX + R_HalfWidth 
R_Top    = R_CenterY + R_HalfHeight 
R_Bottom = R_CenterY - R_HalfHeight 
For a circle host C: 
C_Center = (C_CenterX, C_CenterY) 
C_Radius = Radius 
The default connection attachment boundary is the visible host boundary unless a later rule explicitly says 
to attach to a padded boundary. 
Rectangle boundary attachment calculation 
Input: rectangle center (rx, ry), half sizes hw and hh, and direction vector d = (dx, dy) from the rectangle 
center toward the other host center. 
If dx = 0 and dy = 0, use fallback direction d = (1, 0). 
tx = hw / abs(dx), when dx != 0; otherwise tx = +Infinity 
ty = hh / abs(dy), when dy != 0; otherwise ty = +Infinity 
k = min(tx, ty) 
RectangleBoundaryPointX = rx + k * dx 
RectangleBoundaryPointY = ry + k * dy 
If RectangleBoundaryPointY = R_Top, HostEdgeName = TopEdge. 
If RectangleBoundaryPointX = R_Right, HostEdgeName = RightEdge. 
If RectangleBoundaryPointY = R_Bottom, HostEdgeName = BottomEdge. 
If RectangleBoundaryPointX = R_Left, HostEdgeName = LeftEdge. 
Rectangle host location parameter 
TopEdge:    t = (JunctionX - R_Left) / (R_Right - R_Left) 
BottomEdge: t = (JunctionX - R_Left) / (R_Right - R_Left) 
LeftEdge:   t = (JunctionY - R_Bottom) / (R_Top - R_Bottom) 
--- PAGE 346 ---
RightEdge:  t = (JunctionY - R_Bottom) / (R_Top - R_Bottom) 
The stored rectangle attachment identity shall include HostItemID, HostItemType, HostLocationType = 
RectangleBoundary, HostEdgeName, and HostLocationParameter = t. 
Circle boundary attachment calculation 
Input: circle center (cx, cy), radius r, and direction vector d = (dx, dy) from the circle center toward the 
other host center. 
If dx = 0 and dy = 0, use fallback direction d = (1, 0). 
Length = sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2) 
ux = dx / Length 
uy = dy / Length 
CircleBoundaryPointX = cx + ux * r 
CircleBoundaryPointY = cy + uy * r 
HostAngleRadians = atan2(CircleBoundaryPointY - cy, CircleBoundaryPointX - cx) 
The stored circle attachment identity shall include HostItemID, HostItemType = Circle, HostLocationType 
= CircleBoundary, and HostLocationParameter = HostAngleRadians. 
Shortest-path endpoint calculation by geometry pair 
The function calculateShortestConnectionJunctions(sourceHost, targetHost) shall return exactly two real 
endpoint coordinates: 
StartJunction = (xs, ys) 
EndJunction   = (xe, ye) 
dx_route = xe - xs 
dy_route = ye - ys 
Pair StartJunction calculation EndJunction calculation 
Rectangle -> Rectangle source rectangle boundary in 
direction target center - source 
center 
target rectangle boundary in 
direction source center - target 
center 
Rectangle -> Circle source rectangle boundary in 
direction target circle center - 
source rectangle center 
target circle boundary in direction 
source rectangle center - target 
circle center 
Circle -> Rectangle source circle boundary in direction 
target rectangle center - source 
circle center 
target rectangle boundary in 
direction source circle center - 
target rectangle center 
Circle -> Circle source circle boundary in direction 
target circle center - source circle 
center 
target circle boundary in direction 
source circle center - target circle 
center 
 
--- PAGE 347 ---
The StartJunction and EndJunction shall become real Square Junction Point items. These two junction 
points shall have their own IDs and shall be persisted or restorable through DiagramConnectionDetails. 
Connection route point calculation 
Let StartJunction = S = (xs, ys) and EndJunction = E = (xe, ye). 
dx = xe - xs 
dy = ye - ys 
alpha = default route split ratio, recommended 0.5 
beta = default secondary route split ratio, recommended 0.5 
One-line route 
RoutePoints = [S, E] 
ConnectionSegments = [(S, E)] 
Two-line route 
Horizontal-first two-line route: 
B1 = (xe, ys) 
RoutePoints = [S, B1, E] 
Vertical-first two-line route: 
B1 = (xs, ye) 
RoutePoints = [S, B1, E] 
Three-line route 
Horizontal-first three-line route: 
P1 = (xs + alpha * dx, ys) 
P2 = (xs + alpha * dx, ye) 
RoutePoints = [S, P1, P2, E] 
Vertical-first three-line route: 
P1 = (xs, ys + alpha * dy) 
P2 = (xe, ys + alpha * dy) 
RoutePoints = [S, P1, P2, E] 
Four-line route 
Horizontal-first four-line route: 
P1 = (xs + alpha * dx, ys) 
P2 = (xs + alpha * dx, ys + beta * dy) 
P3 = (xe, ys + beta * dy) 
--- PAGE 348 ---
RoutePoints = [S, P1, P2, P3, E] 
Vertical-first four-line route: 
P1 = (xs, ys + alpha * dy) 
P2 = (xs + beta * dx, ys + alpha * dy) 
P3 = (xs + beta * dx, ye) 
RoutePoints = [S, P1, P2, P3, E] 
The route orientation may be chosen by user selection, default connection type, or the shortest 
uncluttered route rule. If no explicit preference exists, the implementation shall choose a deterministic 
default and keep it stable for reload. 
Intermediate route-point persistence rule 
Intermediate route points are derived route points by default. They shall be recalculated from 
StartJunction, EndJunction, route type, and route parameters. They shall not be persisted as real Square 
Junction Point items unless a later milestone explicitly introduces editable bend handles. 
Connection label position calculations 
The label system shall display text near both real junction points and near the middle of the rendered 
route. Label placement shall use world-space route points, then convert to screen space for rendering. 
LabelOffset = default positive world-space offset from the line, configurable later if needed. 
For any segment A = (x1, y1) to B = (x2, y2): 
vx = x2 - x1 
vy = y2 - y1 
Length = sqrt(vx^2 + vy^2) 
ux = vx / Length 
uy = vy / Length 
nx = -uy 
ny = ux 
Source junction text 
Use the first rendered segment from RoutePoints[0] to RoutePoints[1]. 
SourceLabelX = xs + nx * LabelOffset 
SourceLabelY = ys + ny * LabelOffset 
Destination junction text 
Use the last rendered segment from RoutePoints[n-2] to RoutePoints[n-1]. 
DestinationLabelX = xe + nx_last * LabelOffset 
DestinationLabelY = ye + ny_last * LabelOffset 
--- PAGE 349 ---
Middle-line text 
For a polyline route, compute each segment length and total route length. 
TotalLength = sum(SegmentLength_i) 
MiddleDistance = TotalLength / 2 
Find the segment where cumulative length first reaches MiddleDistance. 
LocalRatio = (MiddleDistance - LengthBeforeSegment) / SegmentLength_current 
MiddleLineTextX = SegmentStartX + LocalRatio * (SegmentEndX - SegmentStartX) 
MiddleLineTextY = SegmentStartY + LocalRatio * (SegmentEndY - SegmentStartY) 
Move recalculation rule 
When a connected host geometry moves, connection-recalculation.js shall recalculate the source or 
destination endpoint using the current host geometry and stored host attachment parameters. If a junction 
point is stored as host-relative, it shall remain attached to the same host boundary location after the host 
moves. 
Junction-point recalculation on resize 
The file junction-point-recalculation-on-resize.js shall run only when a connected host resize can change 
the boundary location that owns the attached junction point. 
Circle resize rule 
Any resize of a connected circle shall recalculate all junction points attached to that circle, because 
Radius changes the full circle boundary. 
Given stored HostAngleRadians = theta: 
JunctionX = CircleCenterX + Radius * cos(theta) 
JunctionY = CircleCenterY + Radius * sin(theta) 
Rectangle resize rule 
A rectangle junction point shall be recalculated only if the selected resize control point changes the edge 
that owns that junction point. 
Attached edge Resize control points that trigger recalculation  
TopEdge TopLeftResizeControlPoint, 
TopCenterResizeControlPoint, 
TopRightResizeControlPoint 
RightEdge TopRightResizeControlPoint, 
RightCenterResizeControlPoint, 
BottomRightResizeControlPoint  
BottomEdge BottomLeftResizeControlPoint, 
BottomCenterResizeControlPoint, 
BottomRightResizeControlPoint  
LeftEdge TopLeftResizeControlPoint, 
LeftCenterResizeControlPoint, 
BottomLeftResizeControlPoint 
--- PAGE 350 ---
 
When the owning edge is affected, rebuild the junction point from HostEdgeName and 
HostLocationParameter t: 
TopEdge:    JunctionX = R_Left + t * (R_Right - R_Left); JunctionY = R_Top 
BottomEdge: JunctionX = R_Left + t * (R_Right - R_Left); JunctionY = R_Bottom 
LeftEdge:   JunctionX = R_Left; JunctionY = R_Bottom + t * (R_Top - R_Bottom) 
RightEdge:  JunctionX = R_Right; JunctionY = R_Bottom + t * (R_Top - R_Bottom) 
If the selected resize control point does not affect the attached edge, junction-point-recalculation-on-
resize.js shall not run for that junction point. 
Database-driven connection recalculation 
When the diagram is loaded or refreshed, DiagramConnections shall identify the connected items and 
DiagramConnectionDetails shall provide route, style, junction, and label information using the shared 
ConnectionID. 
If stored junction point coordinates are available and still valid on their host boundaries, the system may 
restore them. If they are missing or invalid, the system shall recalculate StartJunction and EndJunction 
from the current source and destination host geometry and update the active in-memory model. 
Database-driven acceptance rule: when a row in DiagramConnections and its matching 
DiagramConnectionDetails row are inserted or updated, a reload or refresh shall update the rendered 
connection without requiring the user to manually recreate it. 
Milestone 7 phases 
Milestone 7 shall be implemented in eight phases. 
Phase 7.1 — Right-click connection workflow and Connect-To mode lock 
Goal 
Detect a valid first geometry, show the geometry context menu, display Connect To, and enter Connect-To 
mode while locking unrelated diagram actions. 
Scope 
• right-click target detection 
• geometry context menu rendering 
• Connect To item display 
• Connect-To mode state creation 
• diagram action lock enforcement 
Function contract deliverables 
• detectRightClickTarget 
• openGeometryContextMenu 
• showConnectToMenuItem 
--- PAGE 351 ---
• enterConnectToMode 
• lockDiagramActionsDuringConnectToMode 
• cancelConnectToMode 
Validation / Preconditions 
• the source target must be a connectable geometry 
• the diagram must not already be in an incompatible modal action 
• the context menu target must have a valid item ID 
Error / failure expectations 
• if target detection fails, do not open Connect-To mode 
• if context menu rendering fails, keep diagram state unchanged 
• if another action is active, reject Connect-To mode safely 
Success criteria 
• Connect-To mode can be entered only from a valid geometry 
• drag/drop/move/resize/delete/paste/duplicate/new-item actions are blocked while Connect-To mode is 
active 
Phase 7.2 — Select the second geometry and retrieve possible connection 
types 
Goal 
Allow left-click selection of the second geometry and retrieve possible connection types using CSV first 
and database second. 
Scope 
• left-click target detection while Connect-To mode is active 
• source/target pair validation 
• CSV lookup 
• database fallback lookup 
• bidirectional lookup

• lookupConnectionTypesFromDatabase 
• resolveBidirectionalConnectionLookup 
--- PAGE 352 ---
Validation / Preconditions 
• Connect-To mode must be active 
• source geometry must still exist 
• target geometry must exist and be connectable 
• CSV or database lookup source must be available 
Error / failure expectations 
• if the second target is invalid, keep Connect-To mode active and show a controlled message 
• if CSV fails and DB is available, fall back to DB 
• if both lookup paths fail, show no-available-connections state 
Success criteria 
• Rectangle to Circle works even if the lookup row is stored as Rectangle, Circle and the user selected 
Circle first 
• AWS VPC to AWS VPC returns IPsec tunnel and VPC Peer link options

Goal

--- PAGE 353 ---
Error / failure expectations 
• if the menu cannot render, keep Connect-To mode active without calculating the line 
• if style defaults are missing, apply safe fallback style 
• if option selection is invalid, do not create a connection 
Success criteria 
• the menu appears after second target selection 
• line calculation waits until a valid option is selected 
• IPsec tunnel defaults to dotted orange thin line 
• AWS VPC peer link defaults to solid black thick line 
Phase 7.4 — Create a single-line shortest connection using junction points  
Goal 
Calculate the shortest valid boundary endpoints, create real source and destination Square Junction Point 
items, and render a single-line connection. 
Scope 
• source boundary attachment calculation 
• destination boundary attachment calculation 
• real junction point creation 
• single-line segment creation 
• initial label placement

Validation / Preconditions

• junction point factory must be available 
Error / failure expectations 
• if endpoint calculation fails, do not create the connection 
• if junction creation fails, roll back the pending connection

Success criteria

Phase 7.5 — Create multi-link connection routes using derived

Calculate two-line, three-line, or four-line routes using derived route points while preserving only two real

Error / failure expectations

permits fallback

Success criteria

Error / failure expectations 
• if a connected item is missing, skip the broken connection and log the issue 
• if a junction point is invalid after resize, recalculate from host parameters 
• if recalculation fails, preserve the last valid committed connection where possible 
Success criteria 
• circle resize recalculates all attached circle junction points 
• rectangle resize recalculates only junction points attached to affected edges

Error / failure expectations 
• if detail row is missing, render using default style when possible 
• if IsDeleted is true, do not render the connection 
• if source or destination item is missing, ignore safely and log error 
Success criteria 
• after reload, the diagram renders the same connections, labels, styles, source/destination relationships, 
and route types without manual recreation 
Connection database-driven test criteria 
1. Insert or seed at least two connectable diagram items in the database. 
2. Insert one row into DiagramConnections where SourceItemID references the first item and 
DestinationItemID references the second item. 
3. Insert one matching row into DiagramConnectionDetails using the same ConnectionID. 
4. Load or refresh the diagram by DiagramID. 
5. The diagram shall retrieve the connection rows, calculate or restore source and destination junction 
points, calculate or restore route geometry, and render the connection. 
6. The diagram shall display SourceJunctionText near the source junction point, DestinationJunctionText 
near the destination junction point, and MiddleLineText near the middle of the route. 
7. If ConnectionType is IPsecTunnel and no row-specific style override exists, the diagram shall render a 
dotted orange thin line. 
8. If ConnectionType is AWSVPCPeerLink and no row-specific style override exists, the diagram shall 
render a solid thick black line. 
9. If LineType, LineColor, LineWidth, SourceJunctionText, DestinationJunctionText, or MiddleLineText 
changes in DiagramConnectionDetails, the diagram shall reflect the update after reload or refresh. 
10. If IsDeleted is true in DiagramConnections, the connection shall not render. 
11. If the source or destination item does not exist, the connection shall be ignored safely and an error 
shall be logged without breaking the diagram. 
--- PAGE 358 ---
Milestone 7 save test criteria 
The Milestone 7 save test shall check only the committed variables introduced by Milestone 7 while also 
running regression validation for all previously approved committed variables from Milestones 1 through 
6. 
• save-to-json.js writes DiagramConnections and DiagramConnectionDetails into the committed JSON 
payload. 
• save-to-db.js writes DiagramConnections and DiagramConnectionDetails through the C# API and 
repository layer. 
• reload from JSON restores source/destination item references, connection type, route type, line style, 
junction point references, and labels. 
• reload from database restores the same visible connection state from ConnectionID-linked records. 
• temporary Connect-To mode state is not persisted as committed diagram truth. 
Final result after Milestone 7 
• Connect-To mode works and locks unrelated actions. 
• Second target selection triggers CSV-first and database-fallback connection lookup. 
• Connection options are shown in a menu before line calculation. 
• Global Variables -> Connections exists and shows default connection styles. 
• IPsec tunnel defaults render as dotted orange thin line. 
• AWS VPC peer link defaults render as solid black thick line. 
• Single-line shortest routes are supported. 
• Two-line, three-line, and four-line routes are supported through derived route points. 
• Source and destination real Square Junction Point endpoints are created or restored. 
• Intermediate multi-link route points are derived route points by default. 
• Source junction text, destination junction text, and middle-line text render correctly. 
• Connections recalculate correctly after connected host movement and relevant resize actions. 
• DiagramConnections and DiagramConnectionDetails persist actual connections and details. 
• Database-driven connection updates appear after reload or refresh without manual recreation. 
Recommended implementation order 
1. Implement Connect-To mode state and action lock. 
2. Implement geometry context menu and Connect To action. 
3. Implement second geometry left-click selection. 
4. Implement CSV lookup and database fallback lookup. 
--- PAGE 359 ---
5. Implement connection options menu. 
6. Implement Global Variables -> Connections default style model. 
7. Implement shortest-path junction point calculation. 
8. Implement real source and destination junction point creation. 
9. Implement one-line connection rendering. 
10. Implement two-line, three-line, and four-line route-point calculation. 
11. Implement source, destination, and middle-line labels. 
12. Implement resize-based and move-based connection recalculation. 
13. Extend save-to-json.js and save-to-db.js for connection persistence. 
14. Implement database-driven reload and refresh tests.

Milestone 8 — Circle On Container Edge Placement and Movement 
Behavior 
Overview 
Milestone 8 defines the implementation behavior for a new geometry: a Circle On Container whose center 
point is constrained to the boundary edge of an allowed rectangle container and can move on any edge of 
--- PAGE 361 ---
that same container. This milestone introduces edge attachment, active-edge movement, dynamic 
nearest-edge projection, mandatory corner-transition behavior, recalculation when the parent container 
moves or resizes, and persistence of the committed Circle On Container state. 
This milestone comes after Milestone 7 because Milestone 7 defines connection behavior using junction 
points and lines. The Circle On Container can later become a connection-related boundary object, port 
marker, or engineering interface point, but this milestone focuses only on defining and moving the circle 
on a container edge. 
The new geometry is not a normal child circle inside a container. A normal child circle must remain fully 
inside the parent container. In this milestone, the circle center is on the parent container edge by design, 
so the implementation must use special edge-attachment rules rather than the normal child-circle 
containment rules. 
Milestone 8 includes the following work: 
• define the Circle On Container model and committed variables 
• attach the circle center to one valid edge of an allowed rectangle container 
• move the circle center across any valid edge using current active HostEdge and EdgeParameterT 
• project mouse movement onto the nearest valid edge of the allowed container 
• support deterministic corner transition between adjacent edges 
• recalculate the circle position when the parent container moves or resizes 
• validate sibling overlap and strict no-touch behavior where applicable 
• save and reload the committed Circle On Container state through JSON and database paths 
Mandatory movement clarification: after the Circle On Container is dropped on an allowed container 
edge, it must be movable across all four edges of that same allowed container. The initial drop edge is 
only the starting active edge; it is not a permanent movement restriction. 
Milestone 8 excludes the following work unless a later milestone explicitly adds it: 
• automatic creation of network connections from the Circle On Container 
• new SVG rendering rules for the circle 
• advanced port labeling 
• multi-edge routing around complex polygon containers 
• normal child containment behavior for a center-on-boundary circle

Prerequisites 
Before Milestone 8 implementation starts, the following prerequisites must be confirmed. 
• Milestone 7 connection workflow and junction-point rules are already defined and do not conflict with the 
new circle geometry. 
• Rectangle Container geometry is already defined, including parent boundaries and containment rules. 
• Circle geometry is already defined, including radius, appearance, hover padding, and protection 
padding. 
• World-space, viewport, and screen-space conversion already works correctly. 
• save-to-json.js and save-to-db.js already exist as the only approved top-level save entry files. 
• Database connection is confirmed if the database save phase is implemented immediately. 
--- PAGE 376 ---
Mandatory existing JavaScript before starting Milestone 8 
JavaScript file that must already 
exist Description 
Reason this JavaScript must 
already be ready and working 
correctly 
canvas-state.js Maintains active diagram and 
item state. 
Edge-attached circles must be 
stored in the active diagram 
model before save. 
render-canvas.js Coordinates canvas rendering. The new circle must be drawn 
during the standard render 
cycle. 
world-to-screen.js Converts world coordinates to 
screen coordinates. 
The Circle On Container center 
is world-space authoritative. 
screen-to-world.js Converts pointer screen 
coordinates to world 
coordinates. 
Pointer movement must be 
projected onto a container edge. 
derive-shape-geometry.js Recalculates derived shape 
geometry. 
Parent boundaries and circle 
padding values must be 
available. 
save-to-json.js Shared JSON save entry file. Milestone 8 must extend the 
shared save path rather than 
create a separate top-level save 
file. 
save-to-db.js Shared database save entry file. Milestone 8 must extend the 
shared database save path 
rather than create a separate 
top-level save file. 
 
Shared save prerequisite. Before starting Milestone 8, the team shall verify that save-to-json.js and save-
to-db.js are already saving all committed variables approved in Milestones 1 to 7 correctly. When 
Milestone 8 introduces Circle On Container variables, the developer shall extend only these same two 
files so the current milestone save test checks the Milestone 8 variables, while regression validation 
confirms that all previous milestone variables are still saved correctly. 
Milestone 8 terminology 
Term Meaning 
Circle On Container A circle whose center is constrained to a rectangle 
container edge. 
Parent Container The rectangle container that owns the Circle On 
Container. 
--- PAGE 377 ---
HostEdge The current active edge on which the circle center 
lies: Top, Right, Bottom, or Left. It can change 
during movement so the circle is not locked to a 
single edge. 
EdgeParameterT Normalized position along the current active edge, 
from 0.0 to 1.0. 
CenterOnBoundary Placement policy where the circle center lies 
exactly on the parent visible boundary. 
Corner Transition Required behavior where moving past an edge 
end changes HostEdge to the adjacent edge. 
Previous Valid Edge Position The last accepted EdgeParameterT and center 
values that passed validation. 
 
Important milestone rules 
• The Circle On Container is a separate geometry behavior and shall not be treated as a normal child 
circle inside a container. 
• The authoritative attachment variables are ParentContainerID, HostEdge, and EdgeParameterT . 
• CenterX and CenterY shall be recalculated from the parent container edge before rendering, validation, 
and save acceptance. 
• The circle center must lie exactly on the selected parent edge, subject to numeric epsilon tolerance. 
• Movement along the edge shall be performed in world space. 
• Mouse coordinates shall be converted from screen space to world space before edge projection. 
• If CornerTransitionEnabled = False, movement is clamped to the current active edge. 
• If CornerTransitionEnabled = True, corner crossing must update HostEdge deterministically. 
• The circle shall move with the parent container because its center is derived from the parent container 
geometry. 
• When the parent container is resized, the circle center shall be recalculated from HostEdge and 
EdgeParameterT. 
• Normal child-circle containment rules shall not reject this geometry merely because the circle extends 
across the parent boundary. 
• Sibling overlap and strict no-touch validation shall still be applied according to the selected validation 
scope. 
• JavaScript handles live drag, projection, preview, validation, and rendering. 
• C# handles trusted committed-state validation and database persistence. 
• Browser JavaScript shall not write directly to the database. 
--- PAGE 378 ---
Core Circle On Container equations used in this milestone  
Parent boundaries 
ParentLeftX = ParentCenterX - ParentHalfWidth 
ParentRightX = ParentCenterX + ParentHalfWidth 
ParentTopY = ParentCenterY + ParentHalfHeight 
ParentBottomY = ParentCenterY - ParentHalfHeight 
Edge equations using EdgeParameterT 
Top edge: CenterX = ParentLeftX + t * (ParentRightX - ParentLeftX); CenterY = ParentTopY 
Right edge: CenterX = ParentRightX; CenterY = ParentTopY - t * (ParentTopY - ParentBottomY) 
Bottom edge: CenterX = ParentRightX - t * (ParentRightX - ParentLeftX); CenterY = ParentBottomY 
Left edge: CenterX = ParentLeftX; CenterY = ParentBottomY + t * (ParentTopY - ParentBottomY) 
Clamping rule 
EdgeParameterT_clamped = min(1, max(0, EdgeParameterT_candidate)) 
Circle Protection Padding 
ProtectionPaddingRadius = Radius * ProtectionPaddingRadiusRatio 
ProtectionPaddedRadius = Radius + ProtectionPaddingRadius 
CSV relationship and drop eligibility rules for Milestone 8  
Milestone 8 shall use device-on-container-edge-rules.csv to define which Circle On Container device 
types are allowed on which container types. The CSV file shall have exactly two columns: device on 
container edge and container. 
device on container edge container 
Internet Gateway VPC 
 
A Circle On Container drop is valid only when the dragged DeviceOnContainerEdgeType and the target 
ParentContainerType match a CSV row. For example, Internet Gateway can be placed on VPC edge, but 
not on Region edge unless Internet Gateway,Region is explicitly added. 
Drop eligibility begins when any part of the dragged Circle On Container overlaps the Hover Padding area 
of a candidate container. After eligibility is detected, the system shall validate the CSV rule, choose the 
nearest valid container edge, project the center to that edge, and then calculate HostEdge and 
EdgeParameterT. 
ZOrder and border-occlusion rules for Milestone 8 
CircleOnContainerZOrder must always be greater than ParentContainerZOrder. Recommended default is 
ParentContainerZOrder + 1. The parent container border line shall not be visible through Circle On 
--- PAGE 379 ---
Container. The renderer must either use an opaque Circle On Container fill or mask/suppress the 
container border segment covered by the circle. 
Additional committed Milestone 8 variables 
Add these committed variables to the Milestone 8 save set: DeviceOnContainerEdgeType, 
ParentContainerType, BorderOcclusionPolicy, and the CSV-derived allowed relationship reference where 
applicable. Candidate hover state, candidate container ID, temporary drag preview values, and invalid 
drop candidates shall remain runtime-only values and shall not be saved as committed diagram truth. 
Mathematical Calculation Rules for Milestone 8 
Milestone 8 calculations derive a circle center from a parent rectangle container edge, project pointer 
movement onto that edge, clamp or transition the edge parameter, validate the accepted candidate, and 
preserve the previous valid position if validation fails. 
Primary JavaScript files for this calculation scope: calculate-edge-circle-center.js, project-pointer-to-
container-edge.js, move-circle-on-container.js, validate-edge-circle-placement.js, recalculate-edge-circles-
on-parent-change.js. 
Container edge segment parameter calculation 
For a horizontal edge, derive t from the candidate X coordinate. For a vertical edge, derive t from the 
candidate Y coordinate. The t formula must match the current active edge orientation defined in this 
milestone. 
• Top edge: t = (PointerWorldX - ParentLeftX) / (ParentRightX - ParentLeftX). 
• Right edge: t = (ParentTopY - PointerWorldY) / (ParentTopY - ParentBottomY). 
• Bottom edge: t = (ParentRightX - PointerWorldX) / (ParentRightX - ParentLeftX). 
• Left edge: t = (PointerWorldY - ParentBottomY) / (ParentTopY - ParentBottomY). 
Candidate center projection/clamping 
After t is calculated, clamp it to the range 0.0 to 1.0 unless corner transition is enabled. The center is then 
recalculated using the current active edge equation. The raw pointer position shall not become the circle 
center unless it lies on the edge after projection. 
Edge movement calculation 
During a drag action, the system shall store PreviousValidEdgeParameterT, PreviousValidCenterX, and 
PreviousValidCenterY. Each processed mouse update creates a candidate t and candidate center. If the 
candidate is valid, apply it and update the previous valid variables. If invalid, restore the previous valid 
values. 
Protection Padding and sibling validation calculation 
The Circle On Container ProtectionPaddedRadius shall be used for overlap checks against other Circle 
On Containers and sibling geometries in the same relevant scope. If strict no-touch is required, touching 
counts as invalid. Normal child-circle containment is not applied because the center intentionally lies on 
the parent edge. 
Milestone 8 phases 
Milestone 8 shall be implemented in 8 phases. 
--- PAGE 380 ---
Phase 8.1 — Define Circle On Container geometry model and committed 
variables 
Goal 
Define the model, variables, default values, database fields, and in-memory state for a circle whose 
center is attached to a rectangle container edge. 
Scope 
• define CircleOnContainerID, ParentContainerID, HostEdge, EdgeParameterT, CenterX, CenterY , 
Radius, appearance values, padding values, movement flags, and previous valid values 
• extend the active diagram model to include an CircleOnContainers collection 
• separate committed variables from derived helper values 
Function contract deliverables 
• createCircleOnContainerModel 
• initializeCircleOnContainerDefaults 
• validateCircleOnContainerModel 
• addCircleOnContainerToDiagramState 
Output 
A normalized Circle On Container model exists in the active diagram state. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when the developer can create an Circle On Container object with valid committed 
variables without rendering or movement errors. 
Phase 8.2 — Attach the circle center to a valid rectangle container edge  
Goal 
Create the first valid center-on-edge placement using ParentContainerID, current active HostEdge, and 
EdgeParameterT. The initial HostEdge is only the starting active edge; it must not prevent later movement 
to other edges of the same allowed container. 
Scope 
• select a rectangle container as parent 
• select the initial active edge of the parent container 
• calculate CenterX and CenterY from edge equations 
• render the circle centered on the current active container edge 
Function contract deliverables 
• selectParentContainerForEdgeCircle 
--- PAGE 381 ---
• selectHostEdge 
• calculateEdgeCircleCenter 
• renderCircleOnContainer 
Output 
The circle appears with its center exactly on one valid parent container edge. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when all four HostEdge values can place the circle center on the correct rectangle 
container edge. 
Phase 8.3 — Move the circle center across any valid edge of the container  
Goal 
Allow the user to drag the circle across any edge of the same allowed container while preserving the 
center-on-boundary constraint. 
Scope 
• convert pointer screen position to world space 
• project pointer world position onto the nearest valid container edge 
• calculate candidate EdgeParameterT 
• clamp candidate t when corner transition is disabled 
• recalculate candidate CenterX and CenterY 
Function contract deliverables 
• beginEdgeCircleMove 
• projectPointerToContainerEdge 
• calculateCandidateEdgeParameterT 
• applyValidatedEdgeCircleCenter 
• endEdgeCircleMove 
Output 
The circle moves smoothly along the full container boundary and cannot leave the allowed container edge 
set. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when dragging does not allow the circle center to drift inside or outside the edge 
line. 
--- PAGE 382 ---
Phase 8.4 — Handle edge endpoints and mandatory corner transition 
behavior 
Goal 
Define deterministic behavior when the user drags the circle to a corner so movement can continue onto 
the adjacent edge. 
Scope 
• corner transition is required; the circle shall not be permanently clamped to the first edge 
• transfer the circle to the adjacent edge at the corner using deterministic clockwise/counterclockwise 
rules 
• update HostEdge and EdgeParameterT consistently whenever the active edge changes 
Function contract deliverables 
• clampEdgeParameterT 
• detectEdgeEndpoint 
• resolveCornerTransition 
• updateHostEdgeAfterCornerTransition 
Output 
Endpoint behavior is deterministic and does not create ambiguous circle positions. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when edge-end behavior is predictable for all four container edges. 
Phase 8.5 — Validate Protection Padding and sibling overlap while moving 
along edge 
Goal 
Validate the moving Circle On Container against other relevant geometries without applying the normal 
child-circle containment rule incorrectly. 
Scope 
• recalculate ProtectionPaddedRadius 
• validate edge-attachment equation 
• validate against sibling Circle On Containers and sibling geometries 
• restore previous valid position if strict no-touch overlap is violated 
Function contract deliverables 
• recalculateEdgeCircleProtectionPadding 
• validateEdgeCircleAttachment 
--- PAGE 383 ---
• validateEdgeCircleSiblingNonOverlap 
• restorePreviousValidEdgePosition 
Output 
Invalid overlap or touch candidates are rejected and the circle returns to the previous valid edge position. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when the circle remains attached to the container edge and does not violate the 
selected sibling overlap rule. 
Phase 8.6 — Resize Circle On Container within global radius constraints  
Goal 
Allow the user to resize a Circle On Container while keeping its Radius inside the globally defined radius 
range for the current active edge length. 
Scope 
This phase is about Circle On Container radius resize only. The resize action shall not resize the parent 
container, and parent container resize shall not automatically resize the Circle On Container. 
Required calculations 
CurrentActiveEdgeLength = ParentRightX - ParentLeftX for Top/Bottom edges, and 
CurrentActiveEdgeLength = ParentTopY - ParentBottomY for Right/Left edges. 
MinimumRadius = CurrentActiveEdgeLength * CircleOnContainerMinimumRadiusRatio. MaximumRadius 
= CurrentActiveEdgeLength * CircleOnContainerMaximumRadiusRatio. 
CandidateRadius is valid only when MinimumRadius <= CandidateRadius <= MaximumRadius. The 
recommended live interaction behavior is to clamp the resize preview to the nearest valid radius 
boundary. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when a Circle On Container can be resized within the allowed radius boundaries, 
cannot be resized below the minimum or above the maximum, and does not resize the parent container. 
Phase 8.7 — Recalculate Circle On Container centers when the parent 
container moves or resizes 
Goal 
Keep Circle On Containers correctly attached after parent container movement or resize. 
Parent container movement or ancestor-subtree movement shall move the Circle On Container with the 
host container. Parent container resize shall recalculate the Circle On Container center from HostEdge 
and EdgeParameterT but shall not automatically change Radius. 
Scope 
• when the parent container moves, recalculate all attached circle centers from the moved parent 
boundaries 
• when the parent container resizes, preserve EdgeParameterT and recalculate CenterX/CenterY 
--- PAGE 384 ---
• validate resulting positions after recalculation 
Function contract deliverables 
• getEdgeCirclesByParentContainer 
• recalculateEdgeCirclesOnParentMove 
• recalculateEdgeCirclesOnParentResize 
• validateEdgeCirclesAfterParentChange 
Output 
All attached circles remain on valid edges of their assigned parent containers after parent movement or 
resize. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when parent movement and resize do not detach or misplace any Circle On 
Container. 
Phase 8.8 — Recalculate committed Circle On Container geometry and 
persist to JSON and database 
Exact variables to save in this phase 
Exact committed Milestone 8 save variables: CircleOnContainerID, ParentContainerID, HostEdge, 
EdgeParameterT, CenterX, CenterY, Radius, ZOrder, FillType, FillColor, LineType, LineColor, LineWidth, 
HoverPaddingRadiusRatio, ProtectionPaddingRadiusRatio, MovementEnabled, CornerTransitionEnabled, 
EdgePlacementPolicy. HostEdge stores the current active edge at the time of save, not a permanent 
single-edge restriction. 
PreviousValidEdgeParameterT, PreviousValidCenterX, and PreviousValidCenterY are movement helper 
values. They may be retained in client runtime state during movement, but they should not be treated as 
permanent committed diagram truth unless the project explicitly decides to persist editor recovery state. 
Save regression rule for this phase 
Milestone 8 adds a new committed circle-on-container variable set. The phase fails if save-to-json.js or 
save-to-db.js omits any committed Milestone 8 variable, or if either save path breaks any committed 
variables from Milestones 1 to 7. 
Save test criteria for this phase 
Verify that save-to-json.js writes every listed Milestone 8 variable into the committed JSON payload, that 
save-to-db.js writes the same committed variable set to the trusted database model, that reload restores 
the circle on the saved current active parent edge at the same EdgeParameterT , and that derived helper 
values are recalculated rather than trusted blindly. 
Goal 
Persist the committed Circle On Container state and rebuild it correctly from JSON and database. 
Scope 
• recalculate CenterX and CenterY from ParentContainerID, HostEdge, and EdgeParameterT before save 
acceptance 
--- PAGE 385 ---
• extend save-to-json.js with the CircleOnContainers collection 
• extend save-to-db.js and C# persistence with CircleOnContainers 
• reload the diagram and reconstruct Circle On Container centers from parent geometry 
Function contract deliverables 
• recalculateCommittedCircleOnContainerGeometry 
• buildCircleOnContainerJsonPayload 
• appendCircleOnContainersToSavePayload 
• validateCircleOnContainerSavePayload 
• saveCircleOnContainersToDatabase 
• loadCircleOnContainersFromDatabase 
• rebuildCircleOnContainersAfterLoad 
Output 
The Circle On Container is saved and reopened correctly through both JSON and database paths. 
Success criteria 
This phase succeeds when a saved diagram reloads the Circle On Container on the same parent 
container edge with the same normalized edge position and valid recalculated world-space center. 
Final result after Milestone 8 
At the end of Milestone 8, the developer should have: 
• a formally defined Circle On Container geometry model 
• a circle whose center can be constrained to any valid edge of an allowed rectangle container 
• any-edge movement using current active HostEdge and EdgeParameterT 
• safe projection from pointer movement to edge-constrained movement 
• deterministic endpoint and mandatory corner-transition behavior 
• recalculation after parent container move or resize 
• Protection Padding and sibling overlap validation adapted for this geometry 
• JSON and database persistence through the shared save files 
Recommended implementation order 
1. Define the CircleOnContainer model and variables. 
2. Attach a circle center to one valid parent container edge as the initial active edge. 
3. Render the Circle On Container. 
4. Implement drag movement across any edge of the same allowed container. 
--- PAGE 386 ---
5. Add endpoint and mandatory corner-transition logic. 
6. Add sibling overlap validation and previous valid position restore. 
7. Recalculate attached circles after parent move/resize. 
8. Extend JSON and database persistence through save-to-json.js and save-to-db.js.

unchanged.

More ideas you can define in Milestone 8 
• support inside-biased and outside-biased placement policies where the circle center is offset from the 
boundary by a controlled amount 
• allow Circle On Containers to act as connection ports in a later connection milestone 
• support edge labels that move with EdgeParameterT 
• support snapping to edge divisions such as quarters or thirds 
• support multiple Circle On Containers on the same edge with automatic spacing 
• support lock-to-corner behavior for special port symbols 
Milestone 9 — Protection Padding Calculation and Containment 
Behavior Inside Rectangle Containers 
Overview 
Milestone 9 defines the first focused implementation milestone for Protection Padding calculation inside 
parent containers. 
For this milestone, all containers shall be treated as: 
Rectangle Containers only 
not circle containers 
This milestone uses the existing document rules that state: 
--- PAGE 388 ---
Protection Padding is the mathematical safety region used for overlap avoidance, spacing rules, child-
inside-container calculations, and containment validation 
all Protection Padding calculations must happen in world space 
contained geometries inside a parent rectangle container must remain fully inside the immediate parent 
container and must not get out of it, overlap its border line, or even touch its border line 
clamped movement is the recommended safe behavior for contained items 
This milestone shall be implemented in 7 phases. 
The first 3 phases apply the containment and Protection Padding logic to: 
a single child rectangle inside a parent rectangle container 
The next 3 phases apply the same containment and Protection Padding logic to: 
a single child circle inside a parent rectangle container 
The last phase ensures that all committed Protection Padding geometry and containment state can be 
recalculated and persisted correctly. 
A core design rule for this milestone is: 
JavaScript is responsible for immediate Protection Padding calculation, live containment validation, drag 
behavior, resize behavior, clamped movement, parent-child reference updates, and live rendering 
C# is responsible only for trusted validation, persistence, API handling, and database communication 
when save/load is required 
Another required persistence rule for this milestone is: ParentContainerID = NULL means the item 
belongs directly to DiagramCanvas root rather than to a visible parent container.

The table below includes the main Milestone 9 working variables. 
Milestone 9 variables 
Minimum readiness rule: Milestone 9 should not be considered ready to begin until the team confirms at 
least this: one contained geometry can already be identified together with its immediate parent container, 
and ParentContainerID = NULL is reserved for direct ownership by DiagramCanvas root. 
Required confirmations: Milestones 3 through 7 are already working; Rectangle Container rules are 
defined; single-child containment equations are agreed; Protection Padding calculation helpers exist for 
Rectangle and Circle items; immediate-parent relationships are available; and parent resize behavior is 
conceptually approved. 
Before Milestone 9 implementation starts, the following prerequisites should be confirmed. 
Prerequisites 
Mandatory existing JavaScript before starting Milestone 9 
Only JavaScript files that must already exist and work correctly before this milestone starts are listed 
here. 
Shared save prerequisite. Before starting Milestone 9, the team shall verify that save-to-json.js and 
save-to-db.js are already saving all committed variables approved in Milestones 1 to 8 correctly. When 
Milestone 9 introduces new committed variables, the developer shall extend only these same two files so 
the current milestone save test checks the Milestone 9 variables, while regression validation confirms that 
all previous milestone variables are still saved correctly.

includes Circle children.

Hover Padding. 
Circle child items must already support 
stable selection before container 
validation is added. 
circle-resize.js 
Handles Circle resize 
workflow and candidate 
radius changes. 
Milestone 9 validates containment during 
Circle resize as well as move. 
derive-shape-geometry.js Provides stable derived 
geometry for child items. 
Containment equations compare padded 
child boundaries against parent 
boundaries. 
--- PAGE 395 ---

protection-padding.js 
Recalculates Rectangle 
Protection Padding 
boundaries from the current 
Rectangle geometry. 
Milestone 9 containment and spacing 
rules depend on Rectangle Protection 
Padding being correct before child 
validation begins.

protection-padding.js 
Recalculates Circle Protection 
Padding radius and derived 
boundaries from the current 
Circle geometry. 
Milestone 9 also validates Circle children 
inside Rectangle Containers, so Circle 
Protection Padding must already be 
stable. 
validate-child-
containment.js 
Validates one child item 
against its immediate parent 
boundaries. 
Milestone 9 is built on immediate-parent 
containment validation and cannot 
continue unless this validation path is 
already ready. 
resize-parent-container.js Expands the parent container 
when a child cannot fit under 
approved rules. 
Milestone 9 includes the parent-growth 
rule when a valid child cannot fit inside 
the current parent boundary.

Recommended implementation rule 
The parent container shall expose a usable interior boundary, and all child containment checks shall use 
the parent inner boundaries together with the child Protection Padding boundaries. This follows the 
document’s recommended internal usable boundary rule and the general containment rule. 
 
Important milestone rules 
Rule 1 — Protection Padding is the containment authority 
For this milestone, containment inside a parent rectangle container shall be determined by: 
the child item’s Protection Padding boundaries 
the parent rectangle container’s inner usable boundaries 
Hover Padding shall not be used for containment.  
Rule 2 — All containment checks shall use boundary equations, not center-only equations 
Containment shall not be decided by checking only whether the child center is inside the parent. 
--- PAGE 397 ---
Instead, containment shall be decided by comparing the full Protection Padding boundaries of the child 
item against the parent inner boundaries. The document explicitly recommends boundary equations 
rather than center-only equations.  
Rule 3 — Strict no-touch shall remain enabled by default 
The child must not: 
get out of the parent 
overlap the parent border line 
even touch the parent border line 
Therefore, the containment inequalities should remain strict, and a small epsilon margin may be used for 
implementation safety. This follows the document’s strict no-touch rule. 
Rule 4 — Parent move shall preserve child-parent reference center 
When the parent rectangle container moves, the child item shall move with it and preserve the same 
reference-center offset. 
Recommended reference-center variables: 
ChildParentOffsetX = ChildCenterX - ParentCenterX 
ChildParentOffsetY = ChildCenterY - ParentCenterY 
If the parent later moves by: 
ΔParentX, ΔParentY 
then the child shall move by the same translation: 
NewChildCenterX = OldChildCenterX + ΔParentX 
NewChildCenterY = OldChildCenterY + ΔParentY 
Equivalent offset-preserving form: 
NewChildCenterX = NewParentCenterX + ChildParentOffsetX 
NewChildCenterY = NewParentCenterY + ChildParentOffsetY 
This is the recommended behavior for your requested “same reference center” rule. 
Rule 5 — Parent resize does not automatically scale the child unless explicitly added later 
For this milestone, resizing the parent rectangle container shall change the available containment area, 
but it shall not automatically scale the child item. 
Instead: 
the child remains geometrically unchanged 
the child is revalidated against the new parent inner boundaries 
if the child becomes invalid under the new parent size, the resize shall be rejected or clamped according 
to the chosen policy 
--- PAGE 398 ---
That is the cleanest behavior for version 1. 
 
Core Protection Padding equations used in this milestone  
Parent rectangle inner usable boundary 
For this milestone, the parent rectangle container uses: 
ParentInnerLeftX 
ParentInnerRightX 
ParentInnerTopY 
ParentInnerBottomY 
In version 1, these may equal the parent visible boundaries, which matches the current document 
recommendation.  
Child rectangle Protection Padding boundaries 
For a child rectangle: 
ChildProtectionLeftX = ChildCenterX - ChildHalfWidth - ChildProtectionPaddingX 
ChildProtectionRightX = ChildCenterX + ChildHalfWidth + ChildProtectionPaddingX 
ChildProtectionTopY = ChildCenterY + ChildHalfHeight + ChildProtectionPaddingY 
ChildProtectionBottomY = ChildCenterY - ChildHalfHeight - ChildProtectionPaddingY 
Containment rule: 
ChildProtectionLeftX > ParentInnerLeftX 
ChildProtectionRightX < ParentInnerRightX 
ChildProtectionTopY < ParentInnerTopY 
ChildProtectionBottomY > ParentInnerBottomY 
This follows the child-rectangle containment section already defined in the file.  
Child rectangle clamped center equations 
If the child rectangle is dragged to a target center position, the safe clamped center is: 
ClampedChildCenterX = clamp( 
TargetChildCenterX, 
ParentInnerLeftX + ChildHalfWidth + ChildProtectionPaddingX + ε, 
ParentInnerRightX - ChildHalfWidth - ChildProtectionPaddingX - ε 
) 
ClampedChildCenterY = clamp( 
TargetChildCenterY, 
ParentInnerBottomY + ChildHalfHeight + ChildProtectionPaddingY + ε, 
--- PAGE 399 ---
ParentInnerTopY - ChildHalfHeight - ChildProtectionPaddingY - ε 
) 
This is the direct practical form of the document’s general clamped movement rule plus strict no-touch 
safety margin. 
Child circle Protection Padding boundaries 
For a child circle: 
ChildProtectionPaddedRadius = ChildCircleRadius + ChildCircleProtectionPadding 
ChildProtectionLeftX = ChildCircleCenterX - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius 
ChildProtectionRightX = ChildCircleCenterX + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius 
ChildProtectionTopY = ChildCircleCenterY + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius 
ChildProtectionBottomY = ChildCircleCenterY - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius 
Containment rule: 
ChildProtectionLeftX > ParentInnerLeftX 
ChildProtectionRightX < ParentInnerRightX 
ChildProtectionTopY < ParentInnerTopY 
ChildProtectionBottomY > ParentInnerBottomY 
This follows the document’s rule that circles inside containers should be converted to protection-padded 
left/right/top/bottom boundaries and then checked using the same rectangle-container logic. 
Child circle clamped center equations 
ClampedChildCircleCenterX = clamp( 
TargetChildCircleCenterX, 
ParentInnerLeftX + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius + ε, 
ParentInnerRightX - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius - ε 
) 
ClampedChildCircleCenterY = clamp( 
TargetChildCircleCenterY, 
ParentInnerBottomY + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius + ε, 
ParentInnerTopY - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius - ε 
) 
 
Mathematical Calculation Rules for Milestone 9 
Milestone 9 calculations validate one child item inside one rectangle container using Protection Padding. 
The child may be a rectangle or circle. The parent container uses an inner usable boundary as the 
containment authority. 
--- PAGE 400 ---
Primary JavaScript files for this calculation scope: validate-child-containment.js, resize-parent-
container.js, recalculate-container-derived-geometry-on-resize.js, apply-validated-center.js, restore-
previous-valid-position.js. 
General containment calculation 
ChildProtectionLeftX > ParentInnerLeftX. ChildProtectionRightX < ParentInnerRightX. 
ChildProtectionTopY < ParentInnerTopY. ChildProtectionBottomY > ParentInnerBottomY . 
Child rectangle center clamp 
ClampedCenterX = clamp(TargetCenterX, ParentInnerLeftX + ChildHalfWidth + ChildProtectionPaddingX 
+ Epsilon, ParentInnerRightX - ChildHalfWidth - ChildProtectionPaddingX - Epsilon). 
ClampedCenterY = clamp(TargetCenterY , ParentInnerBottomY + ChildHalfHeight + 
ChildProtectionPaddingY + Epsilon, ParentInnerTopY - ChildHalfHeight - ChildProtectionPaddingY - 
Epsilon). 
Child circle center clamp 
ClampedCenterX = clamp(TargetCenterX, ParentInnerLeftX + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius + Epsilon, 
ParentInnerRightX - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius - Epsilon). 
ClampedCenterY = clamp(TargetCenterY , ParentInnerBottomY + ChildProtectionPaddedRadius + 
Epsilon, ParentInnerTopY - ChildProtectionPaddedRadius - Epsilon). 
Parent expansion calculation 
If insertion or resize requires parent growth: NewParentInnerLeftX = min(CurrentParentInnerLeftX, 
ChildProtectionLeftX - Epsilon), and apply the corresponding max/min expansion to right, top, and bottom 
boundaries. 
 
Milestone 9 phases 
Milestone 9 shall be implemented in 8 phases.

movement under Protection Padding 
For more information, please refer to section Protection Padding, Overlap, Containment, and Persistence 
Calculations (place in this document).

Allow a single child rectangle to move freely inside a parent rectangle container without ever getting out of 
it. 
Scope 
This phase is about: 
a single parent rectangle container 
a single child rectangle 
--- PAGE 401 ---
free child movement inside the parent

clamped containment validation

Expected result: 
the child rectangle moves smoothly 
the child rectangle never gets out of the parent 
the child rectangle never touches the parent border line 
the child rectangle’s Protection Padding boundaries remain valid during movement

the child rectangle never touches the border line

Phase 9.3 — Parent rectangle resize and parent-child reference-center 
preservation for child rectangle 
For more information, please refer to section Parent-container expansion calculations (place in this 
document) and section Protection Padding, Overlap, Containment, and Persistence Calculations (place in

Goal 
Resize the parent rectangle container and then move the child rectangle and the parent container while 
preserving the child-parent reference-center relationship. 
Scope 
This phase is about: 
Whenever parent container resize changes the container geometry, recalculate-container-derived-
geometry-on-resize.js shall run so that visible boundaries, Hover Padding, Protection Padding, inner 
usable boundaries, and containment helper boundaries are recalculated before child-preservation or 
further validation. 
parent rectangle container resize 
revalidation of child rectangle against the resized parent 
parent move with child-follow behavior 
preserving child-parent offset 
Required test behaviors 
Expected result: 
resizing the parent changes the available containment area 
the child remains valid inside the resized parent or the parent resize is rejected/clamped 
when the parent moves, the child moves with it 
the child preserves the same reference-center offset relative to the parent 
--- PAGE 408 ---

ParentResizeCandidate

ChildParentOffsetY

ParentMoveDeltaY

→ applyValidatedParentResize 
→ renderCanvas

left click and drag parent rectangle container 
→ computeParentMoveDelta 
→ moveChildRectangleWithParentReference 
→ applyValidatedParentMove 
→ renderCanvas 
State rule 
During this phase: 
the parent may resize or move 
the child remains associated with the same parent 
when the parent moves, the child moves with it by translation and preserves its reference-center offset 
Success criteria 
This phase is successful if: 
resizing the parent is validated against the current child rectangle 
moving the parent also moves the child rectangle 
the child keeps the same reference-center offset relative to the parent

return valid or invalid parent resize result 
Output: 
valid / invalid parent resize validation result 
State Change / Persistence Effect: 
no persistence effect 
Next Possible Call(s): 
applyValidatedParentResize() 
restorePreviousValidParentGeometry() 
Error Output / Failure Path: 
reject the parent resize if the current child circle would become invalid 
Function Name: moveChildCircleWithParentReference 
Layer: JavaScript 
File: parent-child-reference-center.js 
Purpose: Move the child circle with the parent rectangle while preserving the stored child-parent 
reference-center offset. 
Trigger: 
validated parent move delta generated during parent drag 
--- PAGE 419 ---
Input: 
NewParentCenterX 
NewParentCenterY 
ChildCircleParentOffsetX 
ChildCircleParentOffsetY 
child circle ID 
Validation / Preconditions: 
parent exists 
child circle exists 
stored child-parent reference offset exists 
Processing Responsibility: 
compute new child circle center from the new parent center and the stored reference offset 
recalculate child circle derived geometry 
preserve the same offset relationship 
Output: 
updated child circle center after parent move 
State Change / Persistence Effect: 
updates temporary in-memory parent and child positions only 
no persistence effect 
Next Possible Call(s): 
recalculateChildCircleProtectionPadding() 
renderCanvas() 
Error Output / Failure Path: 
preserve previous valid parent and child positions if reference-offset computation fails 
 
Phase 9.7 — Recalculate committed Protection Padding geometry and 
persist the containment state to JSON and database  
Exact variables to save in this phase 
Milestone 9 introduces no new independent permanent persistence field names. The exact save 
requirement in this phase is: keep saving all previously approved Milestone 2, Milestone 3, Milestone 7, 
and Milestone 8 committed variables, together with the already approved ParentContainerID relationships 
on Device and Container records, after containment recalculation has been accepted. 
Do not save Milestone 9 recalculation or validation helpers as milestone-required persisted data: 
ParentInnerLeftX, ParentInnerRightX, ParentInnerTopY, ParentInnerBottomY , 
CandidateContainedCenterX, CandidateContainedCenterY, ContainedProtectionBounds, 
PreviousValidCenterX, PreviousValidCenterY, ContainmentValidationResult. These values shall be 
recalculated or used only for validation. 
Save regression rule for this phase 
All approved Milestones 2 through 8 committed variables shall already remain saved correctly in both 
persistence paths. This phase passes only if save-to-json.js and save-to-db.js keep the prior approved 
--- PAGE 420 ---
save set intact while persisting the accepted containment result through canonical geometry and parent 
relations only. 
Save test criteria for this phase 
Verify that both shared save files persist the accepted canonical parent and child records with the correct 
ParentContainerID relations, that the recalculation helpers listed above are excluded as milestone-
required saved data, and that reopen from either source restores the same containment result. 
For more information, please refer to section Protection Padding, Overlap, Containment, and Persistence 
Calculations (place in this document). 
Goal 
Recalculate all committed Protection Padding geometry for parent-child containment and persist the 
diagram to both JSON and database. 
Scope 
This phase is about: 
recalculating committed child Protection Padding geometry 
revalidating parent-child containment 
rebuilding the save model 
saving the diagram to JSON 
saving the diagram to the database 
Important recalculation rule 
Before save, the system shall recalculate from the committed geometry: 
parent inner usable boundaries 
child rectangle Protection Padding boundaries if the child is a rectangle 
child circle protection-padded radius and protection-padded boundaries if the child is a circle 
child-parent reference-center offsets if they are part of the committed containment relationship 
This follows the document’s general rule that derived geometry shall be recalculated from canonical 
stored values and that Protection Padding is the authoritative safety region for containment validation. 
Required event-to-function routing 
Save-commit flow 
click Save button 
→ recalculateAllContainmentProtectionPaddingGeometry 
→ validateCommittedContainmentState 
→ buildDiagramJsonObject 
→ serializeDiagramToJson 
→ saveDiagramJsonFile 
→ buildDiagramSavePayload 
→ callSaveDiagramApi 
--- PAGE 421 ---
→ DiagramController.SaveDiagram 
→ DiagramService.SaveDiagram 
→ DiagramRepository.SaveDiagram 
→ handleSaveCommitResult 
State rule 
During this phase: 
recalculation must occur before persistence 
JSON save and database save must use the same committed recalculated containment state 
Success criteria 
This phase is successful if: 
all committed parent-child Protection Padding geometry is recalculated before save 
the saved JSON and database state both represent the same committed containment logic 
reopening from file or database restores the same parent-child containment state 
Function interaction contracts
